**Source:** Shadowrun, Fifth Edition Core Rulebook | `Source/PDF/shadowrunfiftheditioncorerulebook_V2.pdf`  
**Purpose:** Mechanics, items, and one-line pointers to story/hooks/gigs. Skip fiction prose and pure flavor.

Full chapters: [INDEX](INDEX.md)

## 05 - Life in the Sixth World

**Story/hooks:** The Sixth World runs on what you pay (mind, self, soul, blood); shadowrunners need fixers, Mr. Johnson, and legwork to survive megacorps, crime, and each other.

### Everything Has a Price

**Story/hooks:** Magic returned on Dec 24, 2011 (UGE 2011, Goblinization 2021); megacorps rule via extraterritoriality and the Corporate Court; augmentations cost Essence (and Magic/Resonance for Awakened/Emerged).

**Mechanics:** None beyond setting context for Essence, SINs (see below).

### SINners and the SINless

**Mechanics:**
- **SIN (System Identification Number):** Required for legal identity, purchases, and most security systems.
- **SINner:** Has a legal SIN; must broadcast it by law.
- **SINless:** No SIN; pushed into shadows; typically use fake SINs (see Street Gear).
- **Criminal SIN:** Felony record; replaces prior SIN; legal but socially toxic (see SINner quality, ch. 08).
- Born SINless if parents lacked SIN; orks/trolls often denied SINs; criminals get Criminal SINs.

### Where to Run

**Story/hooks:** UCAS/Seattle is the shadowrunning capital; CAS hosts Lone Star; Aztlan is Aztechnology; Amazonia under Hualpa; Japan hosts MCT/Renraku/Shiawase; Hong Kong is Wuxing's cutthroat sprawl; S-K and Lofwyr rule Essen; Denver is five-nation treaty city under Ghostwalker.

**Mechanics:** None.

### A Day in Your Life

**Story/hooks:** You need runner teammates, contacts (especially a fixer), and Mr. Johnson; runs follow meet, legwork, plan, action, wrap-up.

**Mechanics:** None.

### What You Might Be Doing

**Story/hooks:** Job types: datasteal, burglary, breaking things (incl. wetwork), extraction/insertion, delivery/smuggling, protection, hooding, misdirection.

**Mechanics:** None.

### The Opposition

**Story/hooks:** AAA corps (Ares #7, Aztechnology #4, Evo #6, Horizon #10, MCT #3, NeoNET #2, Renraku #5, S-K #1, Shiawase #8, Wuxing #9); organized crime (Mafia, Yakuza/MCT, Triads, Vory, Koshari); street/go-gangs; academics; mana chasers (Draco/Atlantean Foundations); politicos (Humanis, neo-anarchists); privatized law (Lone Star, Knight Errant, etc.); shadow networks (JackPoint, Denver Nexus, Black Lodge, New Revolution).

**Mechanics:** None.

### Off the Job

**Story/hooks:** Downtime covers nuyen/credsticks, Matrix entertainment, music/trideo/sports, soy-based food, commodified sex, VITAS/HMHVV/drugs/BTLs, DocWagon vs street docs, GridGuide vs manual driving, and tiered travel security (semiballistic tightest, regular air loosest).

**Mechanics:**
- **Lifestyle reference:** Full costs on p. 373 (see ch. 08 Lifestyle Costs Table for monthly rates).
- **Dwarf Lifestyle:** +20% cost (metatype table).
- **Troll Lifestyle:** +100% cost (metatype table; gear also +100% at chargen).
- **Certified credsticks:** Offline certified nuyen storage.
- **Corporate scrip:** Megacorp-only currency; lower exchange value.
- **Long trips:** SIN checked; train scanners cheap/foolable; semiballistic requires SIN scan, weapons checked, cyberware deactivated.

## 06 - Shadowrun Concepts

**Story/hooks:** Rules resolve actions and move the story; players and GM cooperate; characters are metatype + attributes + skills + qualities + gear.

**Mechanics:**

### Hits, Thresholds, and Buying Hits

- Roll **D6** dice pools; **5-6 = hit**.
- Meet or beat **threshold** to succeed; excess = **net hits**.
- **Success Test Thresholds:** Easy 1 | Average 2 | Hard 4 | Very Hard 6 | Extreme 8-10.
- **Buying hits:** 1 hit per 4 dice (round down); all dice or roll all; GM approval; avoid if glitch risk matters.

### Glitches

- **Glitch:** More than half dice show 1 (not cancelled by success); complication at GM discretion.
- **Critical glitch:** Glitch + zero hits; serious setback (Edge Close Call can downgrade/negate glitch only, not lack of hits).
- **Extended test glitch:** GM may reduce accumulated hits by 1D6; critical glitch = automatic failure, work lost.

### Tests and Limits

Four test types: **Success (Simple)**, **Opposed**, **Extended**, **Teamwork**.

**Notation examples:**
- `Perception + Intuition [Mental] (2) Test`
- `Sneaking + Agility [Physical] Opposed Test`
- `Automotive Mechanic + Logic [Mental] (10, 1 hour) Extended Test`

**Inherent limits:** Physical, Mental, Social. Cap hits usable on skill+attribute tests. Gear limits (e.g. weapon Accuracy) override inherent limits when applicable. Single-attribute or two-attribute tests use no limits. Edge Push the Limit ignores limit for one test.

**Limit formulas (see Final Calculations, ch. 08):**
- Mental: `[(Logic x 2) + Intuition + Willpower] / 3` (round up)
- Physical: `[(Strength x 2) + Body + Reaction] / 3` (round up)
- Social: `[(Charisma x 2) + Willpower + Essence] / 3` (round up; round Essence up first)

### Extended Tests

- Accumulate hits over intervals; each roll loses 1 die from pool.
- **Extended Thresholds:** Easy 6 | Average 12 | Hard 18 | Very Hard 24 | Extreme 30+.
- **Intervals:** Fast 1 CT | Quick 1 min | Short 10 min | Average 30 min | Long 1 hr | Consuming 1 day | Exhaustive 1 week | Mammoth 1 month.
- Can pause and resume with remaining hits.
- **Rounding:** Round up unless a rule says otherwise.

### Teamwork Tests

- Leader rolls skill + attribute; assistants roll same (or default Intuition - 1 if no skill).
- Each assistant with ≥1 hit: +1 to leader's limit and +1 die to leader's pool.
- Max bonus dice = leader's skill rating (or highest attribute if two-attribute test).
- Assistant glitch: no limit bonus; critical glitch: leader gets no limit bonus.

### Trying Again

- Retry allowed at cumulative **-2** per retry; sufficient break resets penalty.
- Repeated attacks in combat are not "retries."

### Time and Combat Structure

- **Combat Turn:** ~3 seconds; each participant acts.
- **Action types:** Free, Simple, Complex (details ch. 11).

### Levels of Play

- Default: experienced runners. **Street-level** and **prime runner** variants adjust Resources, Karma, gear caps (see ch. 08 Alternate Gameplay).

### Metatypes (overview)

Human, elf, dwarf, ork, troll. Full table in ch. 08.

### Attributes

**Physical:** Body, Agility, Reaction, Strength.  
**Mental:** Willpower, Logic, Intuition, Charisma.  
**Special:** Essence (starts 6), Edge, Magic, Resonance (0 unless Awakened/Emerged).

- Human mental/physical attrs typically 1-6 (mods/qualities can exceed).
- **Essence:** Loss from augmentations; affects Social limit; caps cyber/bio; reduces Magic/Resonance.
- **Edge:** Luck pool; spent for effects (below); attribute unchanged when spending points.

### Initiative and Condition Monitors

- **Initiative:** Reaction + Intuition (+ Initiative Dice, default 1D6, max 5 total dice from effects).
- **Physical CM:** `[Body / 2] + 8` (round up).
- **Stun CM:** `[Willpower / 2] + 8` (round up).
- **Wound modifier:** -1 to all tests per filled row of 3 boxes (stacks).

### Skills (overview)

- Dice pool usually **skill + linked attribute**.
- **Defaulting:** Linked attribute - 1 dice; some skills cannot default (italics on skill list).
- **Qualities:** Positive cost Karma; Negative grant Karma (ch. 08).

### Edge

- Spend 1 Edge per test/action (max 1 per test); only on own actions.
- Can spend Edge on each roll of an Extended test.
- **Push the Limit:** Add Edge rating to pool (before/after roll); ignore limit; Edge dice use Rule of Six (6 = hit, reroll die).
- **Second Chance:** Reroll non-hits; no Rule of Six; no limit change; cannot negate glitch.
- **Seize the Initiative:** Top of order this Combat Turn (ties by Initiative Score).
- **Blitz:** Roll 5 Initiative Dice this turn.
- **Close Call:** Negate glitch, or downgrade critical glitch to glitch.
- **Dead Man's Trigger:** When about to fall unconscious/die, Body + Willpower (3); on success, one final action before blackout.
- **Regaining Edge:** 1 after good meal + 8 hr sleep; GM may refresh 1 for good play (not above max).
- **Burning Edge (permanent -1 Edge attribute):**
  - **Smackdown:** Auto-succeed with 4 net hits (must be capable action; limits ignored).
  - **Not Dead Yet:** Survive otherwise fatal event (still hurt; GM sets circumstances).

## 07 - Girls With Guns

**Story/hooks:** A dwarf runner recalls his first job (Predator from Hauser, van driver) and recruits a hungry street girl after they survive a ganger shootout at Allen's Diner.

**Mechanics:** None (fiction).

## 08 - Creating A Shadowrunner

**Story/hooks:** Build a unique runner in nine steps; default 25 starting Karma; archetypes (street samurai, covert ops, occult investigator, street shaman, combat mage, etc.) provided as samples.

**Mechanics:**

### General

- All characters start with **25 Karma** (customization, qualities, later purchases).
- **Priority System:** Assign A-E once each across five columns (no duplicates): Metatype | Attributes | Magic/Resonance | Skills | Resources.
- Higher priority letter = more valuable in that column.

### Alternate Gameplay

**Street-Level:**
- Resources: A 75,000¥ | B 50,000¥ | C 25,000¥ | D 15,000¥ | E 6,000¥
- Karma: 13 (max 26 total)
- Gear: Device Rating ≤4; Availability ≤10
- Nuyen: max 5 Karma → nuyen (10,000¥)
- Other chargen rules unchanged

**Prime Runner:**
- Resources: A 500,000¥ | B 325,000¥ | C 210,000¥ | D 150,000¥ | E 100,000¥
- Karma: 35 (max 70)
- Gear: Device Rating ≤6; Availability ≤15
- Nuyen: max 25 Karma → nuyen (50,000¥)
- Contacts: Charisma x 6 Karma (not x 3)
- May initiate/submerge at creation

### Step One: Concept

Common roles: Face, Spellcaster, Decker, Technomancer, Rigger, Street Samurai.

### Step Two: Metatype and Attributes

**Priority Table (Metatype / Attributes / Magic or Resonance / Skills / Resources):**

| Priority | Metatype | Attributes | Magic or Resonance | Skills | Resources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | Human (9) Elf (8) Dwarf (7) Ork (7) Troll (5) | 24 | **Magician/Mystic Adept:** Magic 6, two Rating 5 Magical skills, 10 spells/rituals/preps **Technomancer:** Resonance 6, three Rating 5 skills (Resonance/Electronics/Cracking), 7 complex forms | 46/10 | 450,000¥ |
| B | Human (7) Elf (6) Dwarf (4) Ork (4) Troll (0) | 20 | **Magician/Mystic Adept:** Magic 4, two Rating 4 Magical skills, 7 spells/rituals/preps **Technomancer:** Resonance 4, three Rating 4 skills, 4 complex forms **Adept:** Magic 6, one Rating 4 Active skill **Aspected Magician:** Magic 5, one Rating 4 Magical skill group | 36/5 | 275,000¥ |
| C | Human (5) Elf (3) Dwarf (1) Ork (0) | 16 | **Magician/Mystic Adept:** Magic 3, 5 spells/rituals/preps **Technomancer:** Resonance 3, three Rating 2 skills, 3 complex forms **Adept:** Magic 4, one Rating 2 Active skill **Aspected Magician:** Magic 3, one Rating 2 Magical skill group | 28/2 | 140,000¥ |
| D | Human (3) Elf (0) | 14 | **Adept:** Magic 2 **Aspected Magician:** Magic 2 | 22/0 | 50,000¥ |
| E | Human (1) | 12 | - | 18/0 | 6,000¥ |

**Special attribute points:** From Metatype column; spend on Edge, Magic, Resonance only. Edge starts at metatype base; Magic/Resonance start 0. Unspent special points lost after chargen. Max Magic/Resonance/Edge usually 6; humans Edge max 7. Lucky or Exceptional Attribute (GM approval, not both) can raise max by 1.

**Metatype Attribute Table (start/max):**

| Race | BOD | AGI | REA | STR | WIL | LOG | INT | CHA | EDG | ESS | Racial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Human | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 2/7 | 6 | - |
| Elf | 1/6 | 2/7 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 3/8 | 1/6 | 6 | Low-light vision |
| Dwarf | 3/8 | 1/6 | 1/5 | 3/8 | 2/7 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 6 | Thermographic vision; +2 dice vs pathogens/toxins; +20% Lifestyle |
| Ork | 4/9 | 1/6 | 1/6 | 3/8 | 1/6 | 1/5 | 1/6 | 1/5 | 1/6 | 6 | Low-light vision |
| Troll | 5/10 | 1/5 | 1/6 | 5/10 | 1/6 | 1/5 | 1/5 | 1/4 | 1/6 | 6 | Thermographic vision; +1 Reach; +1 dermal armor; +100% Lifestyle |

- **Attribute points:** 1 point = +1 rating from racial start. All attribute points must be spent. Only **one** Mental or Physical attribute at natural max at chargen (Magic/Edge/Resonance exempt).
- **Initiative:** REA + INT (+ dice).

### Step Three: Magic or Resonance

**Magic user types:**

| Type | Key rules |
| --- | --- |
| **Adept** | Power Points = Magic; no astral projection; Assensing only with Astral Perception power; no Sorcery/Conjuring/Enchanting skill groups |
| **Magician** | Astral perceive/project; all magical skills; spells/rituals/preps max known = Magic x 2 each category at creation |
| **Aspected Magician** | One skill group only (Sorcery, Conjuring, or Enchanting); astral perceive not project; same formula cap as magician |
| **Mystic Adept** | Spells like magician; Power Points cost 5 Karma each (max = Magic); no free PP; no astral project (Astral Perception via adept power) |
| **Technomancer** | Resonance attribute; Resonance skills; Living Persona (ch. 13) |

- Priority grants starting Magic/Resonance, skills, spells/complex forms (already paid).
- **Mystic adept Power Points:** 5 Karma per full PP at Step Seven.
- May spend Karma for extra spells, complex forms, bound spirits, registered sprites (Step Seven).

### Step Four: Qualities

- Max **25 Karma** positive and **25 Karma** negative at creation.
- After creation: buy positive at listed x 2; remove negative at bonus x 2 (GM permission).

**Positive Qualities:**

| Analytical Mind | 5 | +2 Logic for pattern/analysis puzzles; half time |

| --- | --- | --- |

**SINner (Layered) types:**
- **National (5):** Legal citizen; 15% gross tax; biometrics in Global SIN Registry.
- **Criminal (10):** Replaces prior SIN; broadcast required; prejudice; 48 hr detention risk; 15% tax; +1 Notoriety.
- **Corporate Limited (15):** Hired wageslave SIN; 20% tax; extraction target; shadow prejudice.
- **Corporate Born (25):** Born corp citizen; 10% tax; deadly liability in shadows if discovered.

**Addiction severity (dose/hour to sate):** Mild monthly -2 Mental or Physical tests in withdrawal | Moderate biweekly -4 | Severe weekly -4 tests, -2 Social always | Burnout daily -6 tests, -3 Social always.

**Allergy severity:** Mild -2 Physical | Moderate -4 Physical | Severe -4 all tests + 1P/min exposure | Extreme -6 all + 1P/30 sec (anaphylaxis).

### Step Five: Purchase Skills

- **Skill points / skill group points:** From Priority Skills column (first number = individual; second = groups).
- 1 point = +1 skill rating OR 1 specialization at creation.
- Max skill/skill group **6** at creation (7 with Aptitude for single skills).
- Skill group purchase = all skills at that rating; breaking group in Step Five not allowed (Step Seven specialization breaks group).
- **Free Knowledge/Language points:** `(Intuition + Logic) x 2`.
- **Native language:** One free (N); Bilingual = second native.
- Max Knowledge/Language **6** at creation.
- Knowledge categories: Academic/Professional (Logic), Interests/Street (Intuition).

**Skill Groups:**

| Group | Skills |
| --- | --- |
| Acting | Con, Impersonation, Performance |
| Athletics | Gymnastics, Running, Swimming |
| Biotech | Biotechnology, Cybertechnology, First Aid, Medicine |
| Close Combat | Blades, Clubs, Unarmed Combat |
| Conjuring | Banishing, Binding, Summoning |
| Cracking | Cybercombat, Electronic Warfare, Hacking |
| Electronics | Computer, Hardware, Software |
| Enchanting | Alchemy, Artificing, Disenchanting |
| Engineering | Aeronautics, Automotive, Industrial, Nautical Mechanic |
| Firearms | Automatics, Longarms, Pistols |
| Influence | Etiquette, Leadership, Negotiation |
| Outdoors | Navigation, Survival, Tracking |
| Sorcery | Counterspelling, Ritual Spellcasting, Spellcasting |
| Stealth | Disguise, Palming, Sneaking |
| Tasking | Compiling, Decompiling, Registering |

**Restricted:** Magic/Resonance skills need Magic/Resonance; aspected magicians one group; deckers cannot take Resonance skills.

### Step Six: Spend Resources

- Spend almost all nuyen; **carry over max 5,000¥** to starting cash (rest lost).
- **Karma to nuyen:** Up to 10 Karma at 2,000¥/Karma (20,000¥ max).
- **Augmentation cap:** +4 total bonus per Mental/Physical attribute from all sources.
- **Chargen gear limits:** Availability ≤12; Device Rating ≤6 (standard).
- **Cyber/bio grades at creation:** Standard, alphaware, used only.
- **Essence loss:** Each full point (or fraction rules) reduces Magic/Resonance by 1 when Essence drops (0.5 loss may not drop Magic until next full point per example).
- Record augmented attributes as `Natural (Augmented)`.

**Lifestyle Costs (monthly):**

| Lifestyle | Cost |
| --- | --- |
| Street | Free |
| Squatter | 500¥ |
| Low | 2,000¥ |
| Middle | 5,000¥ |
| High | 10,000¥ |
| Luxury | 100,000¥ |

**Starting Nuyen (after gear; roll + carryover ≤5,000¥):**

| Lifestyle | Starting Nuyen |
| --- | --- |
| Street | 1D6 x 20¥ |
| Squatter | 2D6 x 40¥ |
| Low | 3D6 x 60¥ |
| Middle | 4D6 x 100¥ |
| High | 5D6 x 500¥ |
| Luxury | 6D6 x 1,000¥ |

### Step Seven: Leftover Karma

- Max **7 Karma** carry into play.
- Cannot initiate/submerge at creation (except prime runner).
- **Contacts (free):** Charisma x 3 Karma for Connection + Loyalty purchases.
- Contact cost: 1 Karma per Connection + 1 per Loyalty (min 2); max 7 Karma on one contact at creation.

**Additional Purchases at Creation:**

| Item | Karma | Restriction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Bound spirits | 1/service | Force = Magic; max spirits = Charisma |
| Complex forms | 4 each | Max = Resonance x 2 |
| Spells | 5 each | Max known = Magic x 2 per category |
| Bond foci | variable | Total Force bonded ≤ Magic x 2 |
| Contacts | see above | - |
| Register sprites | 1/task | Level = Resonance; max = Charisma |

### Step Eight: Final Calculations

| Mechanic | Formula |
| --- | --- |
| Physical Initiative | (Intuition + Reaction) + Initiative Dice |
| Astral Initiative | (Intuition x 2) + 2D6 |
| Matrix AR Initiative | (Intuition + Reaction) + 1D6 |
| Matrix VR Cold Sim | (Data Processing + Intuition) + 3D6 |
| Matrix VR Hot Sim | (Data Processing + Intuition) + 4D6 |
| Mental Limit | see above |
| Physical Limit | see above |
| Social Limit | see above |
| Physical CM | [Body/2] + 8 (round up) |
| Stun CM | [Willpower/2] + 8 (round up) |
| Overflow | Body + augmentation bonuses |
| Living Persona | Attack CHA; Data Processing LOG; Device Rating RES; Firewall WIL; Sleaze INT |

### Step Nine: Final Touches

GM approval; backstory.

### Character Advancement (post-creation)

- **Attributes:** New Rating x 5 Karma; max +2 ratings per downtime; training New Rating x 1 week (not reducible).
- **Active skills:** New Rating x 2; max +3 ratings per downtime.
- **Knowledge/Language:** New Rating x 1.
- **Skill groups:** New Rating x 5; +1 rating per downtime only.
- **Complex form:** 4 Karma | **Spell:** 5 Karma | **Specialization:** 7 Karma | **New Knowledge/Language:** 1 Karma
- **Initiate grade:** 10 + (Grade x 3) | **Submersion grade:** 10 + (Grade x 3)
- **Positive quality (play):** cost x 2 | **Remove negative:** bonus x 2
- **Training rates:** Skill 1-4: New Rating days | 5-8: New Rating weeks | 9-13: New Rating x 2 weeks | Skill group: New Rating x 2 weeks | Specialization: 1 month
- Downtime: one Mental + one Physical attribute OR one attribute + one skill; OR up to Logic/2 (round up) skills if only skills; Dependents +50% time; instructor -25% skill time (round down).
- No attribute training same downtime as aug implant to same attributes.

**Karma Advancement Table (Attributes)** and **Skills** (cumulative totals): see ch. 08 tables (Active skill to 13 with Aptitude; Edge no training time).

## 09 - Skills

**Story/hooks:** Skills turn information into action; specializations narrow expertise; Knowledge defines who you are beyond combat stats.

**Mechanics:**

### Skill Types

- **Active:** Do things (combat, social, magic, Matrix, etc.).
- **Knowledge:** Know things (Academic, Professional, Interests, Street).
- **Language:** Speak/read/write; separate skill per language.

### Skill Groups

- Buy group rating = all component skills at that rating.
- **Breaking a group:** Raise one skill alone; others stay at former group rating; must equalize all skills before re-forming group.
- **Advancement:** Group New Rating x 5 Karma; only +1 per downtime; cannot train other skills same downtime.
- Specializations **not** allowed on groups.

### Skill Ratings

| Rating | Level |
| --- | --- |
| No rating | Unaware (Incompetent; cannot default) |
| 0 | Untrained (may default) |
| 1 | Beginner |
| 2 | Novice |
| 3 | Competent |
| 4 | Proficient |
| 5 | Skilled |
| 6 | Professional (PC max at creation) |
| 7-12 | Veteran through Apex |
| 13 | Apex (Aptitude only) |

- Pool = **skill + linked attribute** (usually).
- Max 12 (13 with Aptitude) for skills; groups max 12.

### Specializations

- **+2 dice** when applicable; one applies at a time.
- Chargen: **1 per skill** max; costs 1 skill point.
- Later: 7 Karma; 1 month training alone.
- Requires skill rating ≥1.

### Defaulting

- Pool = **linked attribute - 1** (+ modifiers).
- Cannot default if skill listed *italic* (no default).
- Edge Push the Limit allowed unless GM says completely lost.
- **Substituting skills:** GM may allow related skill at penalty (e.g. Artisan -3 for Disguise). Active can substitute for Knowledge at penalty; never reverse.

## 10 - Where There's Smoke

**Story/hooks:** Charlie Broken Horse and Ladybug of the Smokers Club abduct Dr. Eric Leiden from a Bellevue cyber clinic to find their missing teammate Riser, fighting corp security and an Awakened troll bodyguard with a water elemental.

## 11 - Combat

### Combat Turn Sequence

1. **Roll Initiative** (all combatants; wound modifiers apply immediately when inflicted).
2. **Begin Initiative Pass** (highest Initiative Score acts first; ties: ERIC = Edge, Reaction, Intuition, coin toss, or act simultaneously).
3. **Begin Action Phase** (acting character; delayed actions declared here):
   - **3A. Declare Actions:** 2 Simple Actions OR 1 Complex Action; 1 Free Action per Action Phase (any time in pass).
   - **3B. Resolve Actions.**
4. **Repeat Step 3** for each character in Initiative order; subtract 10 from all Initiative Scores; repeat Step 2 while any score > 0.
5. **New Combat Turn** when all scores <= 0.

**Late entry:** Roll Initiative, subtract 10 per Initiative Pass already completed.

**Timed items:** Usually detonate on attacker's Initiative Score next Combat Turn (or GM discretion).

### Initiative

| Initiative Type | Attributes | Base Initiative Dice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Physical | Reaction + Intuition | 1D6 |
| Astral | Intuition x 2 | 2D6 |
| Matrix: AR | Reaction + Intuition | 1D6 |
| Matrix: cold-sim VR | Data Processing + Intuition | 3D6 |
| Matrix: hot-sim VR | Data Processing + Intuition | 4D6 |
| Rigging AR | Reaction + Intuition | 1D6 |

**Initiative Score** = Initiative Attribute + Initiative Test (roll Initiative Dice). Max 5D6 with Edge Blitz.

**Initiative Passes:** After each pass, subtract 10; score > 0 = another Action Phase. Score <= 0: one Free Action only (may still defend).

**Changing Initiative:** Attribute change applies difference to current score immediately. Extra dice rolled and added; lost dice rolled and subtracted. Wound modifiers apply to Initiative attribute immediately.

**Edge (Initiative):**
- **Seize the Initiative:** Top of order entire Combat Turn (multiple Edge users go by score, then others).
- **Blitz:** Roll max 5 Initiative Dice for one Combat Turn.

**Delaying Actions:** Declare in Step 3A; act at lower score; -1 dice pool; keep initial score; if not acted before pass ends, still subtract 10.

### Movement

| Metatype | Walk Rate | Run Rate | Sprint Increase |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Dwarf, Troll | Agility x 2 | Agility x 4 | +1m/hit |
| Elf, Human, Ork | Agility x 2 | Agility x 4 | +2m/hit |

**Running:** Exceed Walk rate = Running (Free Action each pass while running); -2 to all actions except Sprint; charge into melee +4 (net +2 with running penalty).

**Sprinting:** Complex Action, Running + Strength [Physical]; max tests = half Running skill (min 1) per Combat Turn.

**Ranged vs movement:** Running target -2; sprinting target -4.

### Combat Actions

**Per Action Phase:** 2 Simple OR 1 Complex + 1 Free. **Interrupt Actions:** Any time if enough Initiative Score remains (not if surprised, except where noted).

#### Free Actions

| Call a Shot | With attack action |
| Change Linked Device Mode | DNI/wireless device mode |
| Drop Object / Drop Prone | Not prone if surprised |
| Eject Smartgun Clip | Smartlinked ready gun |
| Multiple Attacks | Split pool; with attack |
| Run | Exceed walk rate |
| Speak/Text/Transmit Phrase | One short phrase |

| Call/Dismiss/Command Spirit | |
| Change Device/Gun Mode | Smartgun = Free |
| Fire Bow / Fire Weapon (SA, SS, BF, FA simple) | One attack Simple only |
| Insert/Remove Clip, Reload (clip) | |
| Observe in Detail | Perception Test |
| Pick Up/Put Down, Quick Draw, Ready Weapon | |
| Reckless Spellcasting, Shift Perception, Stand Up | Body + Will (2) if wounded prone |
| Take Aim / Take Cover | Not if surprised |
| Throw Weapon, Use Simple Device | |

| Action | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| Astral Projection, Banish Spirit, Cast Spell, Summoning | |
| Fire FA (10 rnd), Long/Semi-Auto Burst | |
| Fire Mounted/Vehicle Weapon, Melee Attack | |
| Matrix actions (most), Rigger Jump In, Sprint | |
| Reload (belt, break, cylinder, drum, internal, speed loader, etc.) | |
| Use Skill | |

| Action | Cost | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Block | -5 Init | +Unarmed Combat to one melee defense |
| Dodge | -5 Init | +Gymnastics to one defense |
| Parry | -5 Init | +melee weapon skill to one melee defense |
| Full Defense | -10 Init | +Willpower to all defenses entire Combat Turn |
| Hit the Dirt | -5 Init | Drop prone vs suppressive fire (no Reaction+Edge test) |
| Intercept | -5 Init | Melee vs mover within 1m + Reach |

#### Reloading

| Method | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Removable clip insert/remove | Simple |
| Speed loader, belt, break, cylinder, drum, internal, muzzle-loader | Complex |
| Bow nock 1 arrow | Simple |

### Combat Resolution (DADA)

1. **Declare:** Attack + defense method (standard Reaction + Intuition free; Dodge/Parry/Block/Full Defense cost Init).
2. **Attack:** Combat Skill + Attribute +/- modifiers [Limit].
3. **Defend:** Reaction + Intuition +/- modifiers; compare hits.
   - Attacker wins: net hits to DV; apply AP to Armor; Physical if modified DV >= modified Armor, else Stun; resist Body + modified Armor (or Body only if Armor 0).
   - Tie: grazing hit (no damage unless contact-only attack applies).
4. **Apply:** Remaining DV to Condition Monitor; wound modifiers; knockdown check.

**Ranged:** Weapon Skill + Agility [Accuracy] vs Reaction + Intuition.

**Melee:** Combat Skill + Agility [Accuracy] vs Reaction + Intuition.

### Accuracy

Melee weapons avg 5; improvised 4; unwieldy 3. Ranged basic 4-5; specialized 6+; improvised 3. Unarmed uses Physical Limit, no Accuracy.

### Armor

**Damage resistance:** Body + Armor (highest worn piece only; +accessories add, max bonus = Strength; every 2 over Strength = -1 Agility and Reaction).

**Physical vs Stun:** If modified DV < AP-modified Armor, Physical becomes Stun.

**AP:** Subtract from target Armor for resistance test.

### Damage

| Track | Formula | Type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Physical | (Body / 2) + 8 | P weapons, most melee, many spells |
| Stun | (Willpower / 2) + 8 | S weapons, blunt, stun ammo |

**Wound modifiers:** Every 3 boxes; apply to all tests except damage resistance and similar; also to Initiative attribute.

**Overflow:** Stun overflow: 2 Stun = 1 Physical. Physical overflow > Body = death (unless stabilized/medical).

### Special Damage

**Electricity:** Stun or Physical; nonconductivity adds to Armor; secondary: -1 all actions and -5 Init for 1 Combat Turn (extend 1 turn per additional damaging hit; no stacking Init/-1). Electronics: Physical only; half DV as Matrix damage. Vehicles: no secondary.

**Fire:** Physical; catch fire test Armor + Fire Resistance - Fire AP vs net hits; burning DV starts 3, +1/Combat Turn; Agility + Intuition reduces DV.

| Fire Type | AP |
| --- | --- |
| Open flame | -2 |
| Fire spell | -Force |
| Flame weapon | -6 |

**Falling:** >3m = Physical DV = meters fallen, AP -4; Body + Armor.

**Fatigue (Stun):** Resist Body + Willpower; not healed while cause persists.

| Source | Interval |
| --- | --- |
| Sprint consecutive passes/turns | 1S cumulative per consecutive sprint |
| Running (not sprint) | Every 3 minutes |
| Mild environment | 6 hours |
| Moderate | 3 hours |
| Harsh | 60 min |
| Extreme | 1 min |
| Deadly | 6 sec |
| Hunger | 6 days |
| Thirst | 2 days |
| Sleep | 3 hours |

### Ranged Modifiers

#### Environmental (use worst; two tied at same severity bump one category)

| Visibility / Light / Wind / Range | Modifier |
| --- | --- |
| Clear / Full / None-Light / Short | - |
| Light / Partial / Light / Medium | -1 |
| Moderate / Dim / Moderate / Long | -3 |
| Heavy / Darkness-Blinding / Strong / Extreme | -6 |
| Two+ at -6 level | -10 |

**Compensation:** Flare comp (glare -2 rows); Image mag (range -1 cat); Low-light (partial/dim as full); Thermo (vis/light -1 row); Tracer FA (wind below light, range below short -1 row); Smartlink (wind -1 row); Sunglasses (glare -1, light -1 down); Ultrasound (vis -1, ignore light within 50m).

#### Recoil

Compensation = 1 free + ceil(Strength/3) + weapon RC - bullets firing. Negative = penalty. Progressive until non-shooting Simple/Complex Action. SS mode: no progressive recoil between SS shots. Vehicle mounts: RC = vehicle Body + weapon RC.

#### Situational (Attacker)

| Situation | Modifier |
| --- | --- |
| Firing from cover with imaging | -3 |
| From moving vehicle (handheld) | -2 |
| In melee | -3 |
| Running | -2 |
| Off-hand | -2 |
| Wounded | -wound |
| Blind fire | -6 |
| Called shot | -4 |
| Take Aim (prior) | +1 dice or +1 Accuracy |
| Wireless smartgun | +1 gear / +2 implanted |

### Firearms

#### Firing Modes

| Mode | Action | Rounds | Defense Mod |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

**Not enough ammo:** Reduce defense modifier 1 per missing bullet; suppressive width -1m per 2 bullets short.

**Suppressive Fire:** Complex, 20 rounds; zone 10m wide x 2m high; (Weapon Skill) + Agility [Accuracy] = penalty to actions in/adjacent zone; enter/leave/move in zone: Reaction + Edge vs hits or take base DV; prone/full cover safe; Hit the Dirt if no Free Action left.

| Medium | Short: -1 DV, -3 def, up to 2 targets/2m; Medium: -3 DV, -3 def, 3/4m; Long: -5 DV, -1 Acc, -3 def, 4/6m; Extreme: -7 DV, -1 Acc, -3 def, 6/8m |
| Wide | Short: -3 DV, -5 def, 2/3m; Medium: -5 DV, -5 def, 3/6m; Long: -7 DV, -1 Acc, -5 def, 4/9m; Extreme: -9 DV, -1 Acc, -5 def, 6/12m |

Medium/wide: no Called Shots.

**Confined space:** Reflect shockwave; combined DV if hit twice.

**Shooting into melee:** If defender beats attacker, allies in melee also defend vs initial hits.

### Surprise

**Test:** Reaction + Intuition (3); alerted +3; ambush +6 (ambushers still test if unaware of target timing).

**Fail:** -10 Init; no defense vs non-surprised; no actions vs them until next Action Phase. Edge: avoid surprise (still -10 Init) but may defend.

**Glitch:** Startle; **Crit glitch:** no first Action Phase, -10 Init.

### Other Combat

**Interception:** Within 1m + Reach; Interrupt -5 Init; damage >= Body stops movement. Avoid: Complex move, Agility + Gymnastics (1) [Physical], each hit passes one opponent.

**Knockdown:** Damage > Physical Limit (gel rounds reduce limit by 2); or 10+ boxes after resist.

**Subduing:** Unarmed hit; compare attacker STR + net hits vs defender Physical Limit; hold = prone; break free Complex Unarmed + STR [Physical] vs grappling net hits.

**Called Shots (-4, Free Action):** Blast out of hands; Dirty Trick (-4 next action); Harder Knock (Stun to Physical); Knock Down (melee); Shake Up (-5 Init); Split Damage (armored, AP < armor); Trick Shot (Intimidation bonus); Vitals (+2 DV).

**Multiple Attacks:** Split pool; max attacks = half Combat Skill.

**Dead Man's Trigger:** Init >=1, spend 1 Edge, Body + Willpower (3); one final action.

### Barriers

| Barrier | Structure | Armor |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fragile (glass) | 1 | 2 |
| Cheap (drywall, door) | 2 | 4 |
| Average (furniture, ballistic glass) | 4 | 6 |
| Heavy (tree, chain link) | 6 | 8 |
| Reinforced (security door, armored glass) | 8 | 12 |
| Structural (brick) | 10 | 16 |
| Heavy structural (concrete) | 12 | 20 |
| Armored/reinforced | 14 | 24 |
| Hardened | 16+ | 32+ |

**Condition Monitor boxes:** Structure rating per square meter (~10 cm thick).

**Penetrating weapons through barrier:** If modified DV > Armor, barrier takes 1 box (2 at 3 bullets, 3 at 6, 4 at 10); remainder to target.

**Body as cover:** Body replaces Structure; Agility/Reaction penalty = Body - holder Strength while carrying.

### Vehicle Combat

**Stats:** Handling, Speed, Acceleration, Body, Armor, Pilot, Sensor.

**Condition Monitor:** 12 + half Body (round up); ignore Stun (electricity = Physical). Drones: 6 + half Body. No damage if modified DV <= modified Armor.

**Vehicle Test:** Vehicle Skill + Reaction [Handling]; threshold by difficulty + terrain modifier.

| Terrain | Modifier |
| --- | --- |
| Open | 0 |
| Light | +1 |
| Restricted | +2 |
| Tight | +4 |

| Threshold | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| 1 Easy | Merge, drift |
| 2 Average | Avoid obstacle, tight turn |
| 3 Hard | Hairpin, unusual terrain |
| 4+ Extreme | Jump obstacle, etc. |

**Jumped in with control rig:** Threshold - rig rating (min 1). AR +1 Handling limit; VR +2; wounded/damaged/visibility/light apply.

**Movement Rates (m/turn):**

| Speed | Walk | Run |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 5 | 10 |
| 2 | 10 | 20 |

| 6 | 160 | 320 |

| 8 | 640 | 1280 |
| 9 | 1280 | 2560 |
| 10 | 2560 | 5120 |

**Ramming:** Melee attack; DV by speed:

| Speed m/turn | DV |
| --- | --- |
| 1-10 | Body/2 |
| 11-50 | Body |
| 51-200 | Body x 2 |
| 201-300 | Body x 3 |
| 301-500 | Body x 5 |
| 501+ | Body x 10 |

Resist Body + Armor - 6 AP; ramming vehicle takes half DV. Control test threshold 2 (rammer) / 3 (rammed) or uncontrolled.

**Crash:** Resist Body of vehicle; Body + Armor - 6 AP; Stun if vehicle Body < character Armor else Physical. Composure (4) or action penalty.

**Chase Combat:** Determine environment (Speed vs Handling), Chase Ranges, Initiative, actions.

| Range | Speed Env (m) | Handling Env (m) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Short | 0-10 | 0-5 |
| Medium | 11-50 | 6-20 |
| Long | 51-150 | 21-80 |
| Extreme | 151-300 | 81-150 |

**Chase Actions (Complex):** Catch-Up/Break Away (Acceleration categories, Reaction + Vehicle [Speed or Handling] vs threshold); Cut-Off (Short, opposed Handling, crash test = net hits); Ram (Short, opposed, damage = rammer Body + net hits, rammer half); Stunt (pursued sets threshold, pursuers match or fall back one range).

**Gunnery:** Gunnery + Agility [Accuracy] manual; Gunnery + Logic [Accuracy] remote. Drone: Pilot + [Weapon] Targeting [Accuracy].

**Sensor targeting:** Passive: Gunnery + Logic [Sensor]; Active: Sensor Test (Simple) then net hits reduce defense.

| Target Size | Sensor Mod |
| --- | --- |
| Large/oversized vehicle | +3 |
| Electric vehicle | -3 |
| Metahuman/critter/drone | -3 |
| Micro-drone | -6 |

**Vehicle defense:** Reaction + Intuition; drone Pilot + autosoft [Handling]. **Evasive Driving:** Free, -10 Init, +Intuition vs attacks (not ram).

**Passengers:** Attack vehicle OR passengers specifically; passengers = Good Cover +3 in moving vehicle; -2 defense inside; add vehicle Armor to personal. Ram/suppression/area affect both.

### Healing

**First Aid:** Complex in combat; First Aid + Logic [Mental] (2); within 1 hour; max heal = skill rating; once per wound set; not after magic heal; full-body armor halves net healing.

**Natural Recovery (Extended):**
- Stun: Body + Willpower (1 hour), 1 box/hit; rest required.
- Physical: Body x 2 (1 day), 1 box/hit; no Physical rest while Stun remains.

**Medicine:** Medicine + Logic [Mental]; +1 die/hit to subsequent natural healing; 30 min/day Physical, 10 min/hour Stun; once per wound set; not in combat.

**Magical Heal:** Per spell rules (ch. 18).

**Stabilization:** First Aid or Medicine + Logic (3); stops overflow bleeding (1 box per Body minutes).

#### Healing Modifiers

| Situation | Modifier |
| --- | --- |
| Good (sterile facility) | +0 |
| Average (indoors) | -1 |
| Poor (street/wilderness) | -2 |
| Bad (combat/weather) | -3 |
| Terrible (fire/storm) | -4 |
| No supplies | -3 |
| Improvised supplies | -1 |

| Remote care | -2 |
| Uncooperative patient | -2 |
| Awakened/Emerged | -2 |
| Implants | -1 per 2 full Essence lost |

## 13 - The Matrix

### Matrix Objects

Persona, device, PAN, file, host, mark. Personas: dwarf-to-troll size protocols. Devices show function in icon. Hosts: large external icon; inside may be much larger (sculpting).

### Grids

| Grid | Access | Penalty |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Public | Global | -2 all Matrix actions (even in hosts) |
| Local | Physical service area only | Normal |
| Global (Big Ten) | Worldwide to 2000 km orbit | Normal |

**Cross-grid actions:** -2 unless Grid-Hop or on same grid. Inside host: no cross-grid penalty.

**Lifestyle default grid:** Low or lower = public; Middle = local; High = global (pick mega); Luxury = any.

### Commlink Matrix Attributes

Data Processing and Firewall = commlink rating (unless custom).

### Matrix Attributes (ASDF)

| Attribute | Role |
| --- | --- |
| Attack | Offensive/harmful code; limit for Attack actions |
| Sleaze | Stealth/probing; limit for Sleaze actions |
| Data Processing | Legal/data actions; VR Initiative component |
| Firewall | Defense; Matrix damage resistance |

Commlinks: DP + Firewall only. Decks/hosts: all four (configurable on deck).

### Device Ratings

| Type | Rating | Examples |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Simple | 1 | Appliances, public terminals |
| Average | 2 | Personal electronics, vehicles, drones, weapons |
| Smart | 3 | Security vehicles, alphaware |
| Advanced | 4 | Betaware, military gear |
| Cutting Edge | 5 | Deltaware, credsticks |
| Bleeding Edge | 6 | Experimental |

### User Modes

| Mode | Initiative | Dice | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AR | Physical | Physical | No biofeedback; -2 Perception if focused on AR |
| Cold-sim VR | DP + Intuition | 3D6 | Biofeedback = Stun |
| Hot-sim VR | DP + Intuition | 4D6 | +2 all Matrix actions; biofeedback = Physical |

Max 5D6 Initiative Dice total.

### Noise

Total noise = distance + situations - reduction; positive noise = dice penalty on actions (not defense/resistance).

| Distance | Noise |
| --- | --- |
| Direct connect | 0 |
| <=100m | 0 |
| 101m-1km | 1 |
| 1-10km | 3 |
| 10-100km | 5 |
| >100km | 8 |

| Situation | Noise |
| --- | --- |
| Dense foliage | 1 per 5m |
| Faraday cage | No signal |
| Fresh water | 1 per 10cm |
| Jamming | 1 per hit |
| Metal-laced earth/wall | 1 per 5m |
| Salt water | 1 per cm |
| Spam/static zone | Rating |
| Wireless negation | Rating |

| Spam Zone | Static Zone | Rating |
| --- | --- | --- |
| City downtown | Abandoned building | 1 |
| Sprawl downtown | Barrens/abandoned underground | 2 |
| Major event/ad blitz | Rural/heavy rain | 3 |
| Commercial city | Wilderness/severe storm | 4 |
| Commercial sprawl | Remote satellite only | 5 |
| Mass gathering/emergency | Remote enclosed (cave) | 6 |

### Matrix Damage

**Monitor:** 8 + (Device Rating / 2) boxes. Resist: Device Rating + Firewall. Persona damage hits hosting device (technomancers: Stun to person).

**Bricking:** Monitor full = device dead until repaired; VR dumpshock if deck bricked.

**Repair:** Hardware + Logic [Mental], 1 hour; each hit removes 1 box OR halves time (min 1 Combat Turn). Crit glitch = permanently bricked.

**Biofeedback:** VR only; cold-sim Stun, hot-sim Physical; resist Willpower + Firewall.

**Dumpshock:** Disconnect VR without Switch Interface Mode: 6S cold / 6P hot; resist Willpower + Firewall; -2 all actions (10 - Willpower) minutes.

**Link-lock:** Cannot Switch Interface Mode, Enter/Exit Host, Reboot; escape Jack Out.

### Illegal Actions and Overwatch

**Illegal:** All Attack and Sleaze actions.

**Failed Attack:** 1 Matrix damage per target defense net hit (unresisted).

**Failed Sleaze:** Target gets mark on you (if not already 3); owner alerted; host launches IC.

**Overwatch Score (OS):** Starts on first illegal action. +target defense hits per illegal action. +2D6 every 15 minutes (GM secret).

**Convergence at OS 40:** 12 DV Matrix damage; forced reboot (dump VR); marks erased; physical location reported to grid owner and host owner.

**Host convergence:** 3 marks on you + IC deploy; leaving host after convergence = immediate grid convergence.

**Check OS:** Electronic Warfare + Logic [Sleaze] vs 6 (Sleaze action adds to OS).

### PANs and WANs

Slave up to (Device Rating x 3) devices to commlink/deck/RCC. Slave uses own or master ratings (whichever higher) on defense; direct connection = no master help.

**Mark propagation:** Mark on slave = mark on master. Failed Sleaze on slave: mark on you from device owner only.

**WAN:** Devices slaved to host; unlimited slaves; in host = direct connection to all WAN devices.

### Matrix Perception / Spotting

| Target | Not silent | Silent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Within 100m | Automatic | Opposed Computer + Intuition [DP] vs Logic + Sleaze |
| Beyond 100m | Simple Computer + Intuition [DP] | |
| Host | Automatic | |

**Running silent:** Simple Action; -2 all Matrix actions. Always know your marks.

**Hide:** Complex; EW + Intuition [Sleaze] vs Intuition + DP; cannot hide from icon that has mark on you.

### Marks

Max 3 per icon (owner = 4 effective marks). Methods: Invite, Brute Force, Hack on the Fly. Erased on reboot or convergence.

**Control Device marks:** 1 Free / 2 Simple / 3 Complex or Standard.

## 15 - Riggers

### Prerequisites

**Control rig** implant (built-in sim module, datajack/cable). **Rigger interface** on vehicle/drone (built into drones). **Skills:** Pilot [Vehicle type], Gunnery.

### Control Priority

Rigger control > remote control (Control Device) > manual > autopilot. Override lasts until Initiative Pass after controller relinquishes.

### Jumping In

**Requires:** Control rig; 3 marks or ownership; rigger interface; VR (Complex from AR, Simple if already VR or direct-connected).

Icons merge in Matrix. **Limits boosted** by control rig rating: Sensor, Speed, Handling, mounted weapon Accuracy.

**VR rigging:** Treat Vehicle actions like Matrix actions (bonuses to Vehicle Control, Gunnery, Sensor tests). Cold-sim: +2D6 Init (3D6 total), Stun biofeedback. Hot-sim: +3D6 Init (4D6 total), +1 all Matrix tests including Vehicle actions, Physical biofeedback.

**Noise:** Wireless rigging suffers noise; direct cable ignores noise.

**Physical damage feedback:** Half (round up) vehicle/drone Physical damage boxes as biofeedback (Willpower + Firewall).

**Matrix damage:** Hits persona device (commlink/RCC) unless direct-connected to jumped-in vehicle (then vehicle takes it).

**Jump out:** Switch Interface Mode; or RCC Jump Into Rigged Device to another slaved drone. **Destroyed while jumped in:** dumpshock 6 DV biofeedback.

### Rigger Command Console (RCC)

**Slaves:** Up to Device Rating x 3 drones. **Noise Reduction + Sharing:** Set at boot; total NR + Sharing <= Device Rating (adjust via Change Device Mode).

**Sharing:** Autosofts on RCC run on all slaved drones simultaneously (if drone not running own autosofts).

**Data Processing:** VR Initiative; Limit for Command tests. **Firewall:** Defends slaved network.

**Group command:** Simple Action command one, all, or subset of slaved drones; executes on drone's Action Phase. Jump drone-to-drone without jumping out. Conflicting commands same pass = none execute, error returned.

**Spider-rigger:** Slave RCC to building host WAN; effective zero distance to host-slaved drones worldwide.

### Electronic Warfare (Riggers)

**Noise compensation:** Complex Action, Electronic Warfare + Logic [DP]; hits = noise reduction rest of Combat Turn (cumulative).

**Defense:** Full Matrix Defense; Reboot Device on hacked drone (offline until end of next Combat Turn).

**Getting dumped:** Vehicle/bricked, RCC/bricked destroyed, or cable yanked = dumpshock + lose control; Pilot resumes next Combat Turn if intact.

### Drones

**Matrix:** Device Rating = Pilot Rating = all Matrix attributes. Jumped in: attacker targets persona/device, not drone.

**Pilot programs:** Device Rating for Mental/Reaction; novel/confusing command: Pilot x 2 vs GM threshold or ignores/stops.

**Autosoft slots:** Half Device Rating (round up). Swap autosoft/program = Complex Matrix Action.

| [Weapon] Targeting | Gunnery for weapon model |

Slaved to RCC without own programs: use RCC autosofts (may exceed slot limit).

RCC programs cannot run on cyberdeck and vice versa; one program per type.

### Drone Operations

**Combat:** Same as character combat (ch. 11). **Gunnery/Sensors:** ch. 11 vehicle rules.

**Perception:** Pilot + Clearsight [Sensor]; jumped in: Perception + Intuition [Sensor].

**Infiltration:** Autonomous: Pilot + Stealth [Handling] vs Perception + Intuition [Mental]. Jumped in: Stealth + Intuition [Handling] vs same. Run silent on drone/persona in Matrix.

**Initiative:** Autonomous: Pilot x 2, 4D6 (3D6 + base 1D6). Jumped in: rigger VR Initiative.

**Repair:** Physical per Build/Repair (ch. 9); Matrix per Matrix Repair rules.

**Damage tracks:** Physical and Matrix; either filled = destroyed/bricked.

## 17 - Magic

*FULL: traditions, sorcery, spirits, adepts, astral, initiation, gear.*


## Introduction

Since the beginning of the Sixth World, certain people have been able to connect to a new force of energy, the stuff called mana. This mana is channeled through a person and projected into spells, or used to summon a being from a metaplane, or to improve their physical capabilities beyond metahumanity's normal limits. The people who can manipulate mana are called "Awakened."

As it turns out, the mana that fills the world is a fragile thing, and its presence ebbs with pollution and human misery. Despite mana's ups and downs, metahumanity has learned to harness it and practice extraordinary feats of magic.

Nowadays, there's a social order among the Awakened. At the top of the food chain are second- and third-generation magicians (people who can cast spells and/or summon spirits) and adepts (people who channel mana into their bodies to perform amazing feats). They've been able to stand on the shoulders of the people who came before them, using magic the same way kids use technology invented in their parents' time""reflexively and intuitively. The knowledge that previous generations struggled to obtain, they simply absorb as quick lessons in their youth. They sometimes have more than a touch of arrogance about them, and sometimes they go to ridiculous lengths to claim a magical heritage for themselves (don't ever get stuck listening to one drone on about how their great-great-great-great-great-great-great grandmother or whatever was burned in Salem as a witch). But they are good at what they do, they have corporations across the world fighting for their services, and they even have their own organizations for people born into "magic families." If they sometimes act as if the world were their plaything, it's because in many ways, it is.

Then you have the mainstream magicians and adepts, magic's equivalent of random lottery winners. One day, often during puberty, they found out that they could make things happen just by thinking about it. They get mad, they glare, and something catches on fire; or they will themselves to incredible feats of physical prowess, leaping over hedges or fences in a single bound, leaving their friends struggling behind.

The newer group of magicians has a caste system of its own""the main divide is between aspected magicians and full magicians. Even though""or possibly because""ninety percent of those given the gift of magic are limited in the ways that they can use it (for example, they can work sorcery only or enchanting only), those who are full magicians believe they are superior to aspected magicians, though it's not like those in the latter group are terribly limited.

Where the ability came from and how they got it is anyone's guess (though several megacorporations are racing to figure out how to intentionally cause someone to Awaken). This group can now have their pick of jobs with corporations""or they can hit the streets and walk around with large targets on their back, thanks to the "geek the mage first" policy every shadowrunner has heard about. Cashing in on the cushy life seems like a no-brainer, but some mages end up on the street anyway. Maybe they've got a bad temper or a criminal record, so corporations don't want to take a risk on them. Maybe they pissed off the wrong people. Or maybe, despite all the pressures of the world, they've managed to hold on to a shred of their integrity and are not ready to sell out. Yet.

All of the Awakened, regardless of their talent level or parentage, have access to the considerable infrastructure of magical industry that has been built over the decades. Curio shops and alchemy stores filled with items of magical interest cater to mages and shamans, carrying competing corporate brand names like Alchemix and Ming Solutions.

Among the items they sell are reagents, small items that carry magical energy and are the magical equivalent of duct tape""useful in just about any situation. They can be used for temporary lodges, spellcasting aids, rituals, even banishing pesky spirits if you have the right combination of ingredients.

### Corporate Lore Stores and Talismongers

| | |
|---|---|
| Ipsissimus (Mitsuhama) | Lore Stores, Inc. (Shiawase) |
| MageWerks (Aztechnology) | Yin and Yang (Wuxing) |
| Pentacles (Ares) | Pentagrams (Manadyne) |

What has remained the same, even after all this time, is the public's ignorance of magic. They're less nervous about magic than they've been in the past, thanks to the desensitizing effects of newsworthy (and, on one occasion, electable) dragons, spirits, fictional and documentary trids, and even ads featuring or catering to mages, but their understanding of how magic works is still very limited.

While magicians and adepts have caught the public's imagination, they've also gotten the attention of the world's various governments and corporate rule-makers, leading to a whole host of regulations covering the use of magic. Anyone with magic ability is supposed to list the fact that they are Awakened on their SINs, and generally they need to possess a license to practice magic as well as a permit for possession of "magic-related paraphernalia." Guilds have to register to be legal, which means they have to prove that all their members are legal magicians. Without registration, they're subject to being raided at any time by Awakened cops on astral patrol, looking for any gathering of Awakened people that may not be authorized. The authorities seem to think that any unapproved discussion between magicians will eventually lead to chaos and/or destruction. They are not entirely wrong.

Most jurisdictions require a license to sell magical goods""maybe just to squeeze out the little independent alchemist""and they throw down a hefty fine on merchants who sell goods to someone who doesn't have the right permits or, spirits forbid, no SIN. Some corporations and governments take their regulation of magic to extreme levels, requiring the Awakened not only to register but also to provide a tissue sample. This means magicians and adepts can be tracked down if they step out of line, or do anything that pisses off the authorities.

So maybe your offense is casting a Clout spell at a Lone Star officer who was annoying you, or maybe it's just not casting a spell when The Man snapped his fingers. Corps see Awakened people as just another resource to exploit. They try to entice magicians to work for them in exchange for a magical education, a better lifestyle, and even a corporate SIN. They're called "wage mages," and while it's not a kind term, a lot of magicians see it as being better than the limited alternatives sitting in front of them.

There are plenty of Awakened who say "Screw that!" and work in the shadows instead. Who wants some ignorant mundane bureaucrat trying to time-manage a ritual? Why should you have to fill out eight forms simply to justify the purchase of frog eyes? Why shouldn't you be able to climb down the side of a building instead of taking the elevator? The corps may have the benefit of being storehouses of magical knowledge, but just because they know stuff doesn't mean that they're going to share. On the streets, things are rough, though for the most part, systems work as you'd expect. Need reagents? Do your legwork, track them down, and either find them yourself or get them in a trade. Want to cast a ritual? You don't have to wait for the higher-ups' approval. You're only limited by your own resourcefulness, stubbornness, and courage. If you're tenacious enough to find more than you need, you're in luck""the shadows are full of potential buyers.

Magic is plenty useful on a run, whether the magician is putting up a shield to deflect bullets or other spells, or concealing the sights and sounds of what a group of runners is doing so they can go about their business undisturbed. Adepts come in handy, too, either launching their bodies into impossible feats or bringing supernatural levels of charm into play. This doesn't come with the same pressure the corps might level on you""instead, you get a completely different range of problems, from the gangs wanting a piece of you to the snipers looking to take you out with a single shot before you can do some serious damage. Then there's law enforcement. The law is well aware that there are Awakened crooks out there, and they have plenty of means at their disposal to deal with them, including specially designed restraints, hoods to prevent them from establishing line of sight so they can cast a spell, and detention rooms treated with substances that inhibit sorcery and conjuring.

The worst part of it is that the authorities have a good reason to be worried about magic, as some spellslingers and adepts are doing some pretty nasty drek. The legal term for illegal magic is maleficium, and it can get pretty ugly. From toxic magic that summons spirits from the bubbling filth of the most polluted corners of the Sixth World to blood magic where casters drain an unsuspecting victim to add power to their spells, magic can go to some twisted places. Most of these practices have an impact on the practitioner, twisting their brains until they are as warped as the magical effects they create. Then they generally either become tyrants of their own small domains or get geeked by runner teams coming out to collect the sizable bounties somebody has inevitably put on their heads.

## Magic Basics

Here's what you need to know to use magic in Shadowrun.

### Magic

Magic is a special Attribute that measures a character's magical power. Characters do not have a Magic Attribute unless they select one of the priorities that specifically provide one (see the Priority Table, p. 65). Magic has a starting value from 1 to 6 (or 7 with the Exceptional Attribute quality), but you don't have to settle for that limit forever. You can go through a process called Initiation (p. 324) that can enhance your abilities. The maximum value of your Magic Attribute (if you have one) is 6 + your Initiation level.

Anything that reduces your Essence also reduces your Magic rating. For every point (or fraction thereof) of Essence lost, both your current Magic Attribute and your maximum Magic Rating are reduced by one. If your Magic is reduced to zero, you can no longer use any skill requiring the Magic attribute, even if your maximum Rating is still greater than zero (but you can still raise the attribute with Karma and then get back to the spellslinging). If your maximum rating falls to zero, you've burned out, losing all magical abilities, including astral perception and projection. You are mundane forever. Burned-out magicians retain all magical skills and knowledge, but they lack the ability to use them. All Magical active skills except for Arcana become Knowledge skills.

### Magical Skills

There are three categories of magical skills. The three Sorcery skills""Spellcasting, Counterspelling, and Ritual Casting""are what you use to manipulate magic in the form of spells. The three Conjuring skills""Summoning, Binding, and Banishing""let you conjure spirits from beyond astral space to aid or serve you. The Enchanting skills""Alchemy, Artificing, and Disenchanting""allow you to imbue mundane objects with magic on the physical plane.

### Force

Almost everything magical has an attribute known as Force, including spirits, spells, foci, and more. This attribute is a measure of magical power of the object or being. Force is used as a limit for magical effects performed without the channeling benefits of reagents or foci.

### Inherent Limit: Astral

If you're going to go messing about in astral space, you need a strong mind, because that's what's going to be doing all your heavy lifting once you leave your body behind. Your mind is both your best muscle and your basic limiter in the astral. Your Astral limit is equal to your Mental or Social limit, whichever is greater.

### Drain

Drain is the price you pay for wielding awesome magical power. The manipulation of mana, whether forming of a spell, brewing a potion, or bringing forth a being from another plane, can exhaust or injure you. Mana is a form of energy, and channeling greater quantities of that energy can strain the body and mind. This effect is referred to as Drain. Magical actions that cause Drain have a Drain Value, which is a measure of how much Drain they inflict.

The base Drain Value for Sorcery and Enchanting tests is listed with the appropriate spell, ritual, or preparation description. For Conjuring tests, the Drain Value is twice the hits (not net hits) generated by the spirit when it resists the Conjuring attempt. Drain generally is inflicted on you as Stun damage, though there are situations in which it can be Physical damage (see the Sorcery, Conjuring, and Enchanting sections). You resist the damage from Drain through a Drain Resistance Test; each hit reduces the Drain by one. Any remaining Drain is applied as boxes of damage to your Stun (or Physical) Condition Monitor. Drain damage, regardless of whether it is Stun or Physical damage, cannot be healed by any means other than the natural properties of the body""that means no magical healing and no medkits. If you overdo it, you'll simply need to make time for some rest.

### Power Points

Power Points are the quantified qi energy that adepts can spend to buy permanent powers. At character creation, adepts receive Power Points equal to their Magic attribute, while mystic adepts have to buy Power Points with Karma. Subsequently, Power Points can come in two ways. If you're an adept, you get a free Power Point whenever you increase your Magic attribute (though this doesn't apply to mystic adepts), and you can gain a Power Point through Initiation (p. 324) instead of gaining a metamagic. For adepts, losing Essence is doubly dangerous because every time your Magic attribute is reduced, you lose an equal number of Power Points (you have to "un-buy" powers to make up the loss). If your Magic attribute is reduced to 0, you are cut off from magic and can no longer access your mystic abilities""which would be a good time for you to avoid anyone you've pissed off in the past while you're trying to learn some new skills.


### Traditions

A magical tradition describes how you interact with mana. Sure, you don't necessarily need things like chants, elegant hand gestures, muttered curses, and a holistic philosophy to sling spells and summon spirits, but it sure helps you connect to the magic, and your tradition gives you exactly that. Often, when looking for ways to focus, people fall back on elements of their culture or background that they associate with magic. If you spend your whole life thinking that magic happens when you wave a small wand, having such a wand in your hand helps you focus your abilities. Or maybe you've been told that magic can be contained in a beaded necklace""in that case, holding and fingering such a necklace is precisely what you need. All magicians are members of one of the magical traditions.

There are many different magic traditions in the Sixth World, and we have two of them for you here. The hermetic tradition values a scholarly, logical approach to spellcasting. Shamanic magic is looser, wilder, and can gain strength from the magician's personality. Followers of the hermetic tradition are called mages, while followers of the shamanic tradition are called shamans.

Different aspects of the world (the elements) are important to traditions in different ways""the description of each tradition describes how each element lines up with the different types of spells, reagents, and spirits in their thinking, indicating which types of spirits and spirit powers they might be likely to call upon in particular situations. Each tradition's description also includes the Attributes used in the Drain Resistance Test for spellcasters of this tradition.

#### The Hermetic Mage

The hermetic mage relies on logic, practice, and execution of a planned formula rather than intuition and improvisation to effectively cast spells. They have learned to control magic and believe that the universe (both the magical parts and the mundane) follows patterns of energy that can be manipulated through complex symbols, formulae, and arcane knowledge of its components. This tradition was widely practiced (if not effective) even before the Awakening, and this form appealed early on to corporations and governments due to its intellectual, formalized nature. In hermetic thinking, casting magic involves imposing the magician's will on the universe""so you can imagine some of the egos that emerge when mages get together.

Mages are scholars and often have libraries of magical information from which they design spells. Any mage worth his reagents has at least a digital copy of one of the founding texts on hermeticism (rich mages have a fancy hard copy written and bound by hand in a very fancy ceremony). Hermetic trappings also include deluxe, well-crafted equipment in archaic laboratories where mages can create preparations and carry out their research.

Mages create circles of power (they're really just magical lodges, just with a hermetic twist, but don't try to tell a mage that). Hermetic reagents include minerals, ores, and other elements""a knowledge of geology, parageology, and chemistry help them find where to gather such reagents. In urban areas, items found in the esoteric, antique, and forgotten corners of the cities can be used by mages. Older buildings, graveyards, and antique shops may have pieces of brick, pottery, glass, wrought iron, and jewelry that have been imbued with magical properties of the elements. Knowledge of architecture and antiques help in the search for these reagents.

Mages, unsurprisingly, take a somewhat arrogant view on conjuring. The mage tends to believe that spirits are intelligent but inferior beings predestined for servitude. With that mindset, mages tend to be more comfortable binding spirits than are spellcasters of other traditions.

**Hermetic Tradition**

| | |
|---|---|
| Combat: Fire | Detection: Air |
| Health: Man | Illusion: Water |
| Manipulation: Earth | Drain: Logic + Willpower |

#### The Shaman

The shaman uses mana to balance the world of nature and their own will. They believe in harmony and guidance, observing the world and using emotion to weave magic. They respect the gift of magic that connects them to the flow of the spirit of life. To the shaman, the earth is a living being, and her children are spirits who guide and aid those that support her; spirits of the sky, mountain, birds, rats, even metahumanity. Shamanism has been practiced far longer than hermetic magic and can be found in all parts of the world, although it wasn't taken seriously by modern metahumanity until the Great Ghost Dance. To the shaman, casting a spell is asking a greater universal entity, such as a mentor spirit, to grant her power.

Shamans call their magical lodges "medicine lodges." A medicine lodge (also known as a sweat lodge, inipi, kiva, temazcal, or a number of other names) can take a variety of forms, including a small domed hut with an animal skin roof or a hole dug into the ground and carefully covered with a ceiling of woven branches, or in an urban area a small room filled with skins, painting, and artistically decorative urban junk.

The wilderness is an excellent place for shamans to gather reagents, finding plant and animal parts, naturally shaped stones, and vials of water in such pristine locations. Knowledge of zoology, parazoology, botany, and parabotany help them find and harvest these materials. In urban areas, shamans find that the spirit of life also inhabits in the cities. So-called "street shamans" find reagents in urban animals, naturally worn stones in parks or waterways, and handcrafted jewelry made with natural materials. Knowledge of architecture and crafts helps find this sort of reagent.

The shamanic perspective on conjuring is based on a belief that spirits are intelligent and ancient, and they should be treated with the respect one would show an elder. Due to their belief that spirits are everywhere, shamans frequently summon spirits from the area when the need arises, rather than binding spirits for long periods. Shamans often develop personal connections to spirits, following them as they learn magic.

**Shamanic Tradition**

| | |
|---|---|
| Combat: Beasts | Detection: Water |
| Health: Earth | Illusion: Air |
| Manipulation: Man | Drain: Charisma + Willpower |

## Magical Lodges

A magical lodge is a stationary collection of symbols, writings, tools, fetishes, and other mystical materials that helps spellcasters record their lore, expand their talent, and practice magic. It's a basic tool of magicians across the world, about the size of a room. You need it to learn spells, perform rituals, craft foci, and in a pinch you can use it to protect yourself from magical threats. Each lodge is of a specific tradition""a shamanic lodge is intended for shamans, and a hermetic lodge for mages.

Magical lodges don't always look like lodges per se. A shamanic lodge is a traditional medicine lodge, but a hermetic magician's lodge is a series of magic circles, shelves filled with arcane tomes, mystical mechanisms, and alchemical apparatuses. Every tradition has its own version of a magical lodge, but they all work the same way.

Magical lodges aren't intended to be portable (although you can make a temporary one with reagents, p. 316). To create your own, you need magical lodge materials (they cost Force x 500 nuyen) that are appropriate to your tradition. Once you have the materials, find a space to put it, like your apartment, or a cave in the wilderness, or a secret abandoned sewer tunnel""someplace you won't mind spending some time. Then spend a number of days equal to the lodge's Force dedicating the space, setting up the physical components, building its astral form, setting up barriers, and harmonizing it to your aura. Once you're done, your magical lodge is up and active. An active magical lodge acts as a mana barrier (p. 315) and shares your astral signature.

If you want to move your lodge, you'll need a day to take it down, turning it back into magical lodge materials; then you can set it up again somewhere else. If you want to improve your lodge, just bring in more materials to add to the current ones and spend a day times the Force you want the lodge to have at the end of your work.

## Perceiving Magic

Magic is rarely subtle. Any form of magic (conjuring, spellcasting, enchanting, magical lodges, spirits, etc.) changes the world around it. Sometimes it's obvious through a magician's gestures or incantations (magicians seen by non-Awakened people are sometimes called "twitchy fingers"). Spirits sometimes cause the air to shimmer, even from astral space. People have reported feeling chills, dread, or other unnatural sensations they can't quite put their finger on when magic is in the area.

Noticing magic is a Simple Perception + Intuition [Mental] Test with a threshold equal to the Skill Rating of the being performing it minus the Force of the magic, or 6 - Force if there's no skill involved (minimum 1 in either case). For example, if a magician with Spellcasting 6 casts a Force 4 manabolt, the threshold for spotting her do it is 2 (Skill Rating 6 - Force 4). If you just stepped through a Force 5 ward, the threshold to notice the markings or feel the tingle is 1 (6 - Force 5). You get a +2 dice pool modifier on this test if you have any magic-related Active or Knowledge Skill.

Obviously, if a magician is throwing fire from his fingertips, you're probably going to notice without making a test. But if the magic is subtle, then you have to pick up some dice.

## Sorcery

Sorcery is the art of shaping mana to create specific effects. You can use it to cast spells (Spellcasting and Ritual Spellcasting), or protect against or destroy spells (Counterspelling). You only have to concentrate to cast a spell, but your tradition probably has plenty of chanting, gestures, dancing, and other things to go along with it. All traditions cast spells using the same rules.

### Spellcasting

Spellcasting is the art of channeling mana through force of will, turning it into an astral or physical form called a spell. Before casting a spell, you have to learn it (Learning Spells, p. 299). Casting a spell involves the following steps:

#### Step 1: Choose Spell

At character creation, magicians purchase a set of spells for spellcasting, then add more as they advance. The magician can only choose from this set of spells.

**Casting Multiple Spells:** Some magicians have the mental discipline to weave multiple threads of mana into various spells at once. You can attempt to cast multiple spells simultaneously in the same action, even at different targets. To do this, you have to split your Spellcasting + Magic dice pool between the spells you want to cast (see Step 4), with a minimum of 1 die per spell. Since the modifiers per spell may be different, they are applied after you split the dice pool. The number of spells you can cast simultaneously is limited to your Magic attribute.

#### Step 2: Choose the Target

The next thing you must do is choose target(s). You need to be able to establish a link with your target""depending on the spell, you'll need to be able to see or touch the target in order to establish the mystical link.

If you need to see the target, line of sight can be established with your natural vision, including using reflective surfaces and looking through transparent objects. Cyber- or bioware visual enhancements that have been paid for with Essence count as natural. Any technological visual aid that digitizes or augments the visual input for you (a camera, electronic binoculars, Matrix feeds, etc.) doesn't work""you're looking at a generated image, not the light from the real target. Spellcasting by visual targeting is subject to normal visibility modifiers. You can use visual targeting to target astral targets when you're in astral space (you're not technically seeing them, but the analogy works).

Some spells can only be cast on targets that you're touching. You don't need to see these targets, but you might need to make an unarmed attack to make contact with an unwilling target. Touching a target through clothing, armor, or a layer of paint is acceptable.

A magician in the physical world can only cast spells on targets that are in the physical world. Similarly, a magician in astral space can only cast spells on targets that are present in astral space (though auras of things in the physical world can be seen, auras alone cannot be targeted). If you're using astral perception (or you are otherwise dual-natured), you can cast spells on targets in either the physical world or astral plane. Only mana-based spells work in astral space, even if you're in the physical world astrally perceiving the target.

**Area Spells:** Area spells can be cast on a specific target or a point in space that you can see. Unless noted in a spell, the area of the spell is a sphere centered on the target with a radius in meters equal to the Force of the spell. All targets in the area of effect that you can see, friend and foe alike, are valid targets for the spell. If a potential target is outside your vision (behind a screen, for example), they're not affected.

#### Step 3: Choose Spell Force

You must declare the Force at which to cast the spell. The Force acts as a limit on the spell. Higher Force spells are more powerful, but cause more Drain. You can cast a spell at a Force up to twice your Magic rating. If the number of hits (not net hits) you get after applying the limit exceeds your Magic rating, the spell's Drain is Physical instead of Stun damage.

#### Step 4: Cast Spell

Casting a spell involves some mental preparation and a Complex Action. Make a Spellcasting + Magic [Force] Test, including dice pool modifiers for injury, sustained spells, and so on. Any opposition roll or threshold you need to reach is listed in the spell description. If the number of hits (not net hits) you get (after applying the limit or Edge spending) exceeds your Magic rating, the spell's Drain is Physical instead of Stun damage.

**Reckless Spellcasting:** You can bypass some of the preparation, but it can take a toll on your body. This action is called Reckless Spellcasting; it lets you cast a spell as a Simple Action instead of a Complex Action, but with an added +3 Drain Value. If you decide to cast two spells as Simple Actions in the same Action Phase, both receive the +3 Drain Value.

#### Step 5: Determine Effect

Details on the type of spell are listed under each spell. Some spells require an Opposed Test prior to resolution, while others can possibly be dodged by the target. Note that this is Step 5, and Drain is Step 6""the effect of the spell happens even if Drain takes you out.

#### Step 6: Resist Drain

Drain calculation is listed for each spell; the Drain Value is determined using the Force and the listed calculation, but can never be lower than 2. After casting a spell, you must resist Drain using the dice pool for Drain Resistance according to your tradition. Remember that if the number of hits you rolled when casting the spell (Step 4) exceeds your Magic rating, then the Drain causes Physical instead of Stun damage.

#### Step 7: Determine Ongoing Effects

If you sustain the spell (that is, if you want the effect of a Sustained spell to continue), you take a -2 dice pool penalty to all tests while it is sustained. You can sustain multiple spells, but the -2 penalty is cumulative for each spell.

Area effect spells that are sustained can be moved as a Complex Action, provided that the spell area is in the magician's line of sight. Characters that drop out of the affected area are no longer affected by the spell, while characters that are enveloped by the new affected area must defend against the spell accordingly.

### Glitches

A glitch on a spell might cause extra drain (generally a +2 Drain Value), invoke a different elemental effect than what was intended, hit the wrong target, or any other bit of mischief your gamemaster can think up. On a critical glitch, the spell could get away from you and cause all sorts of havoc, or you might not get to resist Drain, or it could go off in an unconventional and unpredictable manner that could harm you or your team""again, your gamemaster gets to decide what happens.

### Spell Characteristics

Each spell is described with a set of characteristics. Here's a rundown of those categories and what the different abbreviations accompanying them mean.

- **Type:** Spells are either Physical spells (P) or Mana spells (M). For a full discussion of the difference between the two, see the Physical vs. Mana sidebar (below).
- **Range:** This describes how close you have to get, or how far away you can be, to cast the spell. The most common range is Line of Sight (LOS), meaning you have to physically see the target (described in Step 2 of spellcasting, p. 281). Some spells require you to Touch (T) the target""if the target is unwilling, you'll need to make an unarmed attack against the target (see Accuracy, p. 168). Some spells are Area spells, affecting an area rather than a single target. These spells are noted with (A) after the range designation. With a few exceptions, the area is a radius in meters equal to the Force of the spell.
- **Damage:** If the spell inflicts damage, this describes the type""either Physical (P) or Stun (S). The Damage Value for the spell is listed in the spell or spell category description.

#### Spell Categories

**Combat spells:** These spells are quick, dirty, and violent. The energy of the spell is used to harm, maim, or otherwise frag the target. Spell energy may be channeled directly into the target, damaging it from within, or it may generate external energy to damage the target from the outside.

**Detection spells:** These spells enhance the senses. They allow the subject of the spell to see or hear over great distances, or grant new sensory abilities. There are also Detection spells to sense the presence of other beings, magic, life, and/or enemies.

**Health spells:** These spells affect the condition and performance of a living body. They can be used to treat or heal injuries, purge poisons or toxins, and increase or decrease Attributes.

**Illusion spells:** These spells can mess with a target's perception. They can be used to deceive, make things invisible, confuse the senses, or provide simple or complex entertainments.

**Manipulation spells:** These spells are used by magicians to alter and shape their environment in a variety of ways. Manipulation spells can control the emotions or actions of a person, move objects, shape, create, or channel energy, or change a target's form or appearance by altering its structure.

#### Physical vs. Mana

**Physical spells:** When using Physical spells, the magician converts the channeled mana to energy and/or tangible material in the physical world. The tangible material is simple""energy or matter such as ice, acid, oxygen, heat, light, etc. This material is also temporary unless sustained, converting back to mana energy when the spell ends. Physical spells can affect both living and non-living objects in the physical world.

**Mana spells:** When using mana spells, the magician can only affect living things with auras or astrally active entities (such as spirits or foci) even in the physical world. Mana spells can affect targets on either the physical or astral planes, but only if you're on the same plane as your target(s).
